Adaptive ROMs address this by dynamically updating the low-dimensional subspace during online simulation, but this introduces extra computational overhead, as frequent basis and sampling-point updates erode the computational advantage over static ROMs. A method is needed to reconcile this efficiency-accuracy tradeoff.

Method: The paper introduces a self-adaptive MOR framework that dynamically constructs and deploys the most appropriate ROM formulation (adaptive or static) on the fly, according to evolving solution characteristics. This aims to exploit the advantages of both adaptive- and static-ROM approaches to achieve optimal computational efficiency and predictive accuracy while reducing reliance on extensive offline training. The framework is developed and evaluated for modeling rotating detonation engines (RDEs).

Result: The framework is developed and evaluated for modeling rotating detonation engines (RDEs), which provide a challenging test environment involving strongly convection-dominated, transient, and multiscale reacting-flow dynamics. (Specific quantitative results are not provided in the abstract.)

Conclusion: The proposed self-adaptive MOR framework seeks to exploit the advantages of both adaptive- and static-ROM approaches, achieving optimal computational efficiency and predictive accuracy while reducing reliance on extensive offline training, as demonstrated in the demanding context of RDE modeling.

Abstract: Projection-based model-order reduction (MOR) offers a promising approach for accelerating expensive full-order model (FOM) by constructing reduced-order models (ROMs) that retain the majority of FOM accuracy while significantly reducing the computational cost. However, conventional static ROMs, often following offline/online training strategy, can exhibit limited predictive capability for convection-dominated problems featuring strongly evolving physics such as shocks. Adaptive ROMs emerge as a promising alternative and address such limitation by dynamically updating the low-dimensional subspace during online simulation to track locally evolving system dynamics, thereby enabling accurate predictions even for shock-dominated flows. However, such online adaptation introduces additional computational overhead, and frequent basis and sampling-point updates can substantially reduce their computational advantage relative to their static counterpart. To reconcile this tradeoff, the current work introduces a self-adaptive MOR framework that dynamically constructs and deploys the most appropriate ROM formulation (either adaptive or static) on the fly according to the evolving characteristics of the solution. to achieve optimal computational efficiency while maintaining high modeling accuracy of the local physics during the online model execution. In this regard, the proposed framework seeks to exploit the advantages of both adaptive- and static-ROM approaches to achieve an optimal computational efficiency and predictive accuracy while reducing reliance on extensive offline training. The framework is developed and evaluated for modeling rotating detonation engines (RDEs), which provide a challenging test environment involving strongly convection-dominated, transient, and multiscale reacting-flow dynamics.

</details>


### [111] [Principles and Techniques for Agentic Research in Experimental Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17193)
*Niko R. Reed,Declan M. Daly,Andrew J. Beling,Jordan D. Giandrea,Ronald L. Walsworth,John W. Blanchard,Daniel Ang*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes ten requirements and an implementation framework for collaborative AI agents in experimental physics, validated through six case studies spanning design, control, optimization, modeling, and data analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: AI use in physics research is increasingly common, yet few guidelines exist for its effective use, particularly for AI agents acting collaboratively in experimental physics.

Method: A principles-based, human-centered approach defining ten requirements for collaborative AI agents across scientific norms, effective agent use, and domain expertise. A framework enforces these via custom scaffolding to improve off-the-shelf LLM behavior, plus general-purpose scientific workflows and skills, emphasizing closed-loop campaigns where agents iterate toward a defined goal.

Result: Six case studies in experimental physics yielded useful work products: a neural network architecture and solid-state quantum defect photodynamic model outperforming published work; a fabrication-ready microwave waveguide design; a quantum control pulse sequence improving simulated performance by over an order of magnitude; NMR spectrometer commissioning to ~1 Hz precision on live hardware; and a data-fitting pipeline achieving the first reconstruction of the Ising microstate of an artificial spin-ice array from diffraction-limited magnetic imaging.

Conclusion: A principles-based, human-centered framework of collaborative AI agents can effectively amplify researcher expertise, as demonstrated across diverse experimental physics tasks with demonstrable utility and performance gains.

Abstract: The use of artificial intelligence (AI) in physics research is increasingly common, yet few guidelines exist for its effective use. Starting with a principles-based approach, we outline a set of ten requirements for collaborative AI agents in experimental physics, spanning scientific norms, effective agent use, and domain expertise. We take a human-centered approach in which agents act as collaborators that amplify rather than replace the expertise of a human researcher (user) who directs the work and is responsible for its results. We then present a framework designed to enforce these requirements, detailing methods for improving off-the-shelf behavior of large language models (LLMs) with custom scaffolding and describing general-purpose scientific workflows and skills. In particular, we highlight the power of closed-loop campaigns where agents work iteratively toward a well-defined goal. We demonstrate the potential of this mode of research through six case studies in experimental physics, covering device design, hardware control, neural network optimization, physical model identification, and data analysis. All six case studies result in useful work products that add utility to our research, including a neural network architecture and solid-state quantum defect photodynamic model that substantially outperform our published work; a microwave waveguide design that extends a published design concept to a fabrication-ready device meeting our experimental constraints; a quantum control pulse sequence candidate that improves simulated performance by more than an order of magnitude; commissioning of an NMR spectrometer to $\sim$1~Hz frequency precision on live hardware; and finally a data-fitting pipeline that achieves, to our knowledge, the first reconstruction of the Ising microstate of an artificial spin-ice array from diffraction-limited magnetic imaging.

</details>


### [112] [Wisevolve Potential Engine: Machine-Learning Molecular Dynamics at 100 Million Atom-Steps per Second on a Single GPU](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17366)
*Hekai Bu,Zheyong Fan*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: WPE accelerates GPU potential evaluation in GPUMD by selecting and reusing a model- and stage-specific GPU execution policy, achieving large speedups across NEP, qNEP, classical, and PIMD workloads while matching reference results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: General-purpose molecular dynamics software must support diverse interatomic potentials and simulation requirements. Within a given simulation stage, the loaded potential model and required calculations typically remain fixed as the atomic configuration evolves, suggesting that model- and stage-specific specialization could be exploited for acceleration. However, general-purpose MD codes often apply generic execution policies rather than specializing to the specific model and stage.

Method: The Wisevolve Potential Engine (WPE) uses information from GPUMD to select and reuse a GPU execution policy tailored to the potential model and simulation stage. GPUMD retains simulation control and ownership of the simulation data, while WPE specializes GPU potential evaluation per model and per stage.

Result: On a single NVIDIA RTX 5090, WPE-enabled GPUMD (GPUMD-WPE) achieves a median throughput of 109 million atom-step/s across 106 NEP workloads, and 142 million atom-step/s for a 15.1-million-atom diamond Si system. Relative to GPUMD, geometric-mean speedups are 3.49x across NEP workloads, 3.61x across 16 qNEP workloads, 1.24x across 28 classical-potential workloads, and 10.5x across 56 PIMD configurations. End-to-end calculations of water structure, lattice thermal conductivity, and PIMD thermal expansion closely agree with GPUMD and published results. A 12.5-million-atom Cu shock-compression simulation demonstrates applicability to large-scale nonequilibrium dynamics.

Conclusion: Model- and stage-specific specialization is a practical way to accelerate GPU potential evaluation in general-purpose molecular dynamics workflows, as demonstrated by WPE's substantial speedups and consistent accuracy across diverse potentials, engine sizes, and simulation types.

Abstract: General-purpose molecular dynamics software supports diverse interatomic potentials and simulation requirements. Within a simulation stage, the loaded potential model and the required calculations typically remain fixed as the atomic configuration evolves. We present the Wisevolve Potential Engine (WPE), which uses this information from GPUMD to select and reuse a GPU execution policy tailored to the model and simulation stage. GPUMD retains simulation control and ownership of the simulation data. On a single NVIDIA RTX 5090, WPE-enabled GPUMD (GPUMD-WPE) achieves a median throughput of 109 million atom-step/s across 106 neuroevolution potential (NEP) workloads. For a 15.1-million-atom diamond Si system, it sustains 142 million atom-step/s. Relative to GPUMD, geometric-mean speedups are 3.49x across these NEP workloads, 3.61x across 16 workloads using NEP with dynamic charges (qNEP), 1.24x across 28 classical-potential workloads, and 10.5x across 56 path-integral molecular dynamics (PIMD) configurations. End-to-end calculations of water structure, lattice thermal conductivity, and PIMD thermal expansion show close agreement with corresponding GPUMD results and published simulation results. A separate 12.5-million-atom Cu shock-compression simulation demonstrates its applicability to large-scale nonequilibrium dynamics. These results support model- and stage-specific specialization as a practical way to accelerate GPU potential evaluation in general-purpose molecular dynamics workflows.

</details>


### [113] [KINTORA: a semi-Lagrangian discontinuous Galerkin drift-kinetic, unstructured mesh tokamak solver](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17428)
*Mark F. Adams*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: A continuum drift-kinetic solver on unstructured triangular poloidal planes extruded toroidally, using semi-Lagrangian discontinuous Galerkin with field-line-following parallel advance (flux-coordinate independent), adiabatic electrons, and a long-wavelength polarization solve; all operators verified within 4% (12% for geodesic-acoustic damping), a toroidal ITG mode with growth rate 0.065 c_s/a recovered, the zonal-flow residual not yet converged, and results reproduced CPU/GPU and across MPI counts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing continuum gyrokinetic codes rely on structured, field-aligned grids on nested flux surfaces, while particle-in-cell codes support unstructured meshes and open field lines but suffer a statistical noise floor. A solver that combines unstructured-mesh flexibility with noise-free continuum discretization, without requiring field alignment, is missing.

Method: A continuum drift-kinetic solver using a semi-Lagrangian discontinuous Galerkin discretization on unstructured triangular poloidal planes extruded toroidally. The parallel advance is a map along the exact field line in a flux-coordinate independent manner, so the mesh is never field-aligned. Adiabatic electrons and a long-wavelength polarization field solve are included. Every operator is verified against independently computed references.

Result: Verification spans Landau damping, the geodesic-acoustic root, and a linear ion-temperature-gradient eigenvalue problem, with every measured rate within 4% except geodesic-acoustic damping (within 12%). A linear toroidal ITG demonstration on a canonical-Maxwellian equilibrium recovers a radially coherent mode with growth rate 0.065 c_s/a, stationary in time. The collisionless zonal-flow residual rises from 0.72 to 0.87 of the model's orbit-quadrature prediction with poloidal refinement and has not converged. The solver is fully device resident; results reproduce between CPU/GPU builds and across MPI process counts.

Conclusion: The solver establishes a viable continuum alternative occupying neither the structured field-aligned nor the PIC position, with operator-level verification and reproducible, portable performance. The unconverged zonal-flow residual indicates remaining refinement sensitivity, and the code, all tabulated data, and an artifact description are released in a public repository.

Abstract: Continuum gyrokinetic codes use structured, field-aligned grids on nested flux surfaces; particle-in-cell codes support unstructured meshes and open field lines but carry a statistical noise floor. We present a continuum drift-kinetic solver that occupies neither position: a semi-Lagrangian discontinuous Galerkin discretization on unstructured triangular poloidal planes extruded toroidally, whose parallel advance is a map along the exact field line in the flux-coordinate independent manner, so the mesh is never aligned with the field, with adiabatic electrons and a long-wavelength polarization field solve. Every operator is verified against an independently computed reference, from Landau damping to the geodesic-acoustic root and a linear ion-temperature-gradient eigenvalue problem, every measured rate within 4% except the geodesic-acoustic damping, within 12%. A linear toroidal ion-temperature-gradient demonstration on a canonical-Maxwellian equilibrium recovers a radially coherent mode whose growth rate, 0.065 c_s/a, is stationary in time. The collisionless zonal-flow residual is the one measurement still moving: it rises with poloidal refinement from 0.72 to 0.87 of the model's own orbit-quadrature prediction for the measured observable and has not converged. The solver is fully device resident, and every result is reproduced between CPU and GPU builds and across MPI process counts. The code, the data behind every table and an artifact description are in a public repository.

</details>


<div id='cond-mat.str-el'></div>

# cond-mat.str-el [[Back]](#toc)

### [114] [Exact SU($N$) symmetry resolution of bosonic Hubbard models: general boson-fermion mappings and flat-band singlet-cluster states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14183)
*Loïc Herviou*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper presents an exact diagonalization method for SU(N)-symmetric Bose-Hubbard models within each irreducible representation of SU(N), leveraging U(L)–U(N) Howe duality, and applies it to finite-temperature phase diagrams and frustrated-lattice singlet-cluster states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: SU(N)-symmetric Bose-Hubbard models are computationally challenging because the Hilbert space grows rapidly and standard diagonalization does not exploit the full symmetry. A method that resolves the SU(N) irreducible representations could reduce the computational burden while revealing exact structural properties of the spectrum.

Method: Exploiting the Howe duality between U(L) and U(N), the authors perform exact diagonalization separately in each irreducible representation of SU(N) for any lattice and any Hamiltonian built from U(L) generators. They introduce a color-resolved diagonalization scheme.

Result: Three structural consequences are established: (i) the spectrum in a given orbital sector is independent of N and of particle statistics; (ii) bosonic and fermionic canonical partition functions satisfy Z_b(−N) = (−1)^{N_b} Z_f(N); (iii) with unfrustrated hopping the ground state is always a color ferromagnet. Applications include the finite-temperature phase diagram of attractive SU(N) bosons on few-site rings at large N for arbitrary filling, and the emergence of singlet-cluster states from frustrated hopping on sawtooth and kagome lattices.

Conclusion: The color-resolved Howe-duality approach provides an efficient and general exact diagonalization framework for SU(N) Bose-Hubbard models, yielding exact spectral identities and enabling the study of excited sectors, frustrated lattices, and finite temperatures; it also reveals singlet-cluster states as the counterpart of fermionic ferromagnetism.

Abstract: We show how to perform exact diagonalization of SU($N$)-symmetric Bose-Hubbard models separately in each irreducible representation of SU($N$), by exploiting the Howe duality between U($L$) and U($N$). Three structural consequences follow for any lattice and any Hamiltonian built from the U($L$) generators: (i) the spectrum in a given orbital sector is independent of $N$ and of the particle statistics, (ii) the canonical partition functions for bosons and fermions obey the exact identity $Z_{\mathrm{b}}(-N)=(-1)^{N_\mathrm{b}}Z_{\mathrm{f}}(N)$, (iii) with unfrustrated hopping the groundstate is always a color ferromagnet, so that the color-resolved method we introduce is useful to study excited sectors, frustrated lattices and finite temperatures. As applications, we first derive the finite-temperature phase diagram of attractive SU($N$) bosons on few-site rings at large $N$ for arbitrary filling. We then show the emergence of singlet-cluster states from frustrated hopping, the counterpart of fermionic ferromagnetism, with the example of the sawtooth and kagome lattice.

</details>


### [115] [Exotic metallic phase in a heterotic Kondo lattice: violation of Luttinger's theorem and absence of Kondo gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14556)
*Hanting Zhong,Shuxiang Yang,Chao Cao,Xiao-Yong Feng,Jianhui Dai,Hai-Qing Lin*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This work studies a heterostructured Kondo lattice in its paramagnetic phase, finding a fractional Dirac liquid in the weak Kondo-coupling regime and a large-Fermi-surface heavy-fermion metal without a Kondo gap in the strong-coupling regime—even at exact half filling, where Luttinger's theorem is violated.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fermi surface transformations accompanying quantum phase transitions are widely observed in correlated electron systems (heavy fermions, van der Waals quantum heterostructures). The unified Anderson lattice description suggests that at electron half filling the interacting ground state always destabilizes the metallic phase in the strong Kondo-coupling regime, motivating a re-examination of this claim in the paramagnetic phase of a Kondo lattice with a heterotic layered structure.

Method: Analysis of the paramagnetic phase of a Kondo lattice system with a heterotic layered structure, examining both weak and strong Kondo-coupling regimes and the dependence on layer stacking configurations.

Result: Weak coupling: a fractional Dirac liquid with massless/massive Dirac fermions and a charge-neutral spinon Fermi surface. Strong coupling: spinons hybridize with Dirac fermions, producing an intermediate nearly-flat hybridizing band plus conduction and valence heavy-fermion bands, giving a large electric Fermi surface. No Kondo gap appears between these bands depending on stacking configuration; the system stays metallic even at exact half filling, and Luttinger's theorem is violated across the critical point.

Conclusion: The half-filling metallic phase survives in the strong Kondo-coupling regime for certain layered stacking configurations, with Luttinger's theorem violated across the transition. These results have implications for van der Waals quantum heterostructures.

Abstract: Fermi surface transformation accompanied by quantum phase transition is widely observed in correlated electron systems including heavy fermion materials and van der Waals quantum heterostructures. The unified Anderson lattice description for this phenomenon suggests that their interacting ground states in the case of electron half filling always destabilize the metallic phase in the strong Kondo-coupling regime. We examine this issue within the paramagnetic phase of a Kondo lattice system with a heterotic layered structure. In the weak Kondo-coupling regime, the system exhibits a fractional Dirac liquid involving the massless or massive Dirac fermions and a charge neutral spinon Fermi surface. In the strong Kondo-coupling regime, the spinons hybridize with the Dirac fermions resulting in an intermediate nearly-flat hybridizing band together with the conduction and valence heavy fermion bands, yielding a large electric Fermi surface. Absence of the Kondo gap between these bands is observed depending on the stacking configurations of the layered structure. In particular, the system remains metallic even at the exact half filling in this regime and Luttinger's theorem is violated across the critical point. Implications of these results on the van der Waals quantum heterostructures are briefly discussed.

</details>


### [116] [Emergent one-dimensional Bloch oscillations in coupled Ising layers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14857)
*E. S. Ma,Z. Song*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Domain-wall excitations in coupled Ising layers under asymmetric longitudinal fields form an emergent 1D quasiparticle subspace with an effective linear potential, producing a Wannier–Stark ladder and Bloch oscillations without any conventional tilted potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bloch oscillations are conventionally understood to require a constant force from a potential gradient in a periodic system. The paper seeks a distinct, unconventional mechanism for coherent Bloch-type dynamics, using domain walls in coupled Ising layers rather than a standard tilted lattice potential.

Method: The authors study a two-dimensional system of coupled Ising layers with Heisenberg-type interlayer coupling, which acts as an effective transverse coupling in a pseudospin (rung) representation. They analyze the kinetic constraints imposed by the rung pseudospin structure and the effective linear potential generated by the field imbalance between layers.

Result: The rung pseudospin structure restricts many-body dynamics to an emergent one-dimensional quasiparticle subspace. The interlayer field asymmetry creates an effective linear potential that confines emergent domain-wall excitations between two oppositely polarized ferromagnetic domains, yielding a Wannier–Stark ladder and Bloch oscillations without a conventional tilted potential. Time-dependent numerical simulations confirm the analytical predictions.

Conclusion: Bloch oscillations can emerge from asymmetric-field-induced confinement of domain walls in coupled Ising layers, revealing a distinct mechanism for coherent quantum dynamics driven by interlayer coupling and field imbalance rather than a conventional potential gradient.

Abstract: Bloch oscillations typically arise in periodic systems subjected to a constant force generated by a potential gradient. Here, we study domain-wall dynamics in coupled Ising layers under asymmetric longitudinal fields. The interlayer coupling is of Heisenberg type and acts as an effective transverse coupling in the emergent pseudospin representation. We show that the rung pseudospin structure gives rise to kinetic constraints, restricting the many-body dynamics to an emergent one-dimensional quasiparticle subspace within the original two-dimensional system. Furthermore, the imbalance between the longitudinal fields of the two layers induces an effective linear potential for the emergent domain-wall excitations, leading to their confinement between two oppositely polarized ferromagnetic domains. Remarkably, the resulting Wannier--Stark ladder of domain-wall excitations gives rise to Bloch oscillations in the absence of a conventional tilted potential, revealing a distinct mechanism for coherent quantum dynamics. Numerical simulations of time-dependent observables confirm the analytical predictions.

</details>


### [117] [Spectral Mapping Theorem between SU(N) and SU(Ns/N) Heisenberg Models and Exact Diagonalization of SU(4) and SU(7) Long-Range Ordered Phases on the Triangular Lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15162)
*Samuel Gozel,Pierre Nataf*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper proves a spectral duality between SU(N) and SU(Ns/N) Heisenberg models via standard Young tableaux transposition, and uses an efficient SYT-based exact diagonalization to study SU(4) and SU(7) Heisenberg models on the triangular lattice up to Ns=28 sites.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The nearest-neighbor ground state of the SU(4) Heisenberg model on the triangular lattice remains debated, and exact diagonalization of SU(N>3) Heisenberg models is limited by the lack of efficient, memory-saving methods for large system sizes and many colors.

Method: The authors establish a theorem that the full singlet-sector spectrum of the SU(N) Heisenberg model with isotropic two-site interactions on Ns sites equals the negative of the singlet-sector spectrum of the SU(Ns/N) model on the same lattice, derived from a transposition operation in the standard Young tableaux basis. They then implement this SYT basis efficiently for large-scale exact diagonalization and apply it to SU(4) and SU(7) Heisenberg models on the triangular lattice up to Ns=28 sites.

Result: For the SU(4) case, next-nearest-neighbor couplings are confirmed to stabilize four-sublattice long-range order. Increasing the number of colors from N=4 to N=7 is also found to provide another route toward a Neel-like ground state, even without additional couplings.

Conclusion: The SYT transposition theorem and its efficient implementation enable unprecedented large-scale ED of SU(N>3) Heisenberg models, revealing that both next-nearest-neighbor couplings in SU(4) and increasing N up to SU(7) can stabilize long-range ordered ground states on the triangular lattice.

Abstract: We first show that, for the SU(N) Heisenberg model with isotropic two-site interactions on an arbitrary lattice of Ns sites, the full spectrum in its singlet sector (when N divides Ns) is the negative of the full spectrum in its singlet sector of the SU(Ns/N) Heisenberg model defined on the same lattice. This theorem, valid for models with the fundamental irreducible representation (irrep) at each lattice site, follows naturally from a transposition operation in the basis of Standard Young Tableaux (SYTs). Second, this basis -- previously recognized as particularly convenient for exact diagonalization (ED) of SU(N) Heisenberg models -- admits an efficient, memory-saving implementation, thereby enabling large-scale diagonalizations for unprecedented system sizes Ns and numbers of colors N>3. We apply these ideas to an ED study of the SU(4) and SU(7) Heisenberg models on the triangular lattice (TL), extending the calculations up to Ns=28 sites. In particular, starting from the SU(4) case, whose nearest-neighbor ground state remains debated, we confirm that next-nearest-neighbor couplings stabilize four-sublattice long-range order. We further find that increasing the number of colors from N=4 to N=7 provides another route toward a Néel-like ground state, even without such additional couplings. N = 4 to N = 7 provides another route toward a Néel-like ground state, even without such additional couplings.

</details>


### [118] [Superlattice-Stabilized Fractional Chern Insulators in a "Berry Basin" Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15197)
*Yongxin Zeng,Andrew J. Millis*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The authors introduce a "Berry basin" model that captures the essential features of rhombohedral graphene (a dispersive band with a flat bottom, momentum-space layer winding, and Coulomb interactions) plus periodic potentials, to study how quantum geometry, superlattice modulation, and interactions jointly produce correlated topological states beyond the isolated topological flat band paradigm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The discovery of integer and fractional Chern insulators in rhombohedral graphene, following their discovery in the isolated topological flat band of twisted MoTe2, motivates a search for the general conditions under which an electronic band can host a topological correlated state. Existing paradigms rely on isolated flat bands, so a broader framework is needed.

Method: A "Berry basin" model is constructed that reproduces key rhombohedral graphene features: a dispersive band with a flat bottom, momentum-space winding of a layer degree of freedom, and Coulomb interactions. Periodic (superlattice) potentials are additionally included, enabling controlled numerical studies of the interplay between quantum geometry, superlattice modulation, and electron-electron interactions.

Result: Integer Chern insulators emerge at strong coupling even without a superlattice potential. In contrast, stabilizing the fractional state requires a nonzero superlattice potential for all interaction strengths accessed. The results explain the greater robustness of integer and fractional Chern insulators in rhombohedral graphene when electrons are polarized away from the moiré interface.

Conclusion: The work establishes a new class of systems hosting robust fractional Chern insulators and provides a controlled numerical platform for testing competing theories of interacting topological phases, extending correlated topological states beyond the isolated topological flat band paradigm.

Abstract: The discovery of integer and fractional Chern insulators in rhombohedral graphene, following their discovery in the isolated topological flat band in twisted MoTe$_2$, motivates a search for the conditions under which an electronic band can host a topological correlated state. In this Letter we introduce a ``Berry basin" model which captures the essential features of rhombohedral graphene: a dispersive band with a flat bottom, momentum-space winding of a ``layer" degree of freedom, and Coulomb interactions, while also including periodic potentials, enabling controlled studies of the interplay of quantum geometry, superlattice modulation, and electron-electron interactions in producing correlated topological states beyond the isolated topological band paradigm. While integer Chern insulators emerge at strong coupling without a superlattice potential, stabilizing the fractional state requires nonzero potential for all interaction strengths we access. We provide insight into the greater robustness of integer and fractional Chern insulators in rhombohedral graphene when electrons are polarized away from the moiré interface. Our results establish a new class of systems that host robust fractional Chern insulators and provide a controlled numerical platform to test competing theories of interacting topological phases.

</details>


### [119] [Dispersion of low-energy optical excitations in NiPS$_3$ explained by singlet polaron theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15230)
*I. J. Hamad,C. S. Helman,A. E. Feiguin,L. O. Manuel,A. A. Aligia*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This work uses singlet-polaron theory to explain the momentum-dependent excitonic excitations observed by RIXS in the van der Waals antiferromagnet NiPS3, showing the lowest-energy singlet polaron dispersion matches experiment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Optical and RIXS experiments on NiPS3 reveal narrow excitonic resonances near 1.5 eV, but their microscopic origin remains under debate. A consistent theoretical interpretation of the momentum dependence of these excitations is needed.

Method: Applying the previously developed singlet-polaron theory (SPT) for NiPS3, the authors calculate the spectral function of the corresponding effective model and extract the quasiparticle dispersion. The theory predicts two singlet excitations whose dynamics arise from propagation in the zig-zag antiferromagnetic background (S=1) coupled to two-magnon processes.

Result: The dispersion of the lowest-energy quasiparticle agrees very well with the excitonic dispersion measured by RIXS, reproducing both its overall shape and bandwidth along high-symmetry directions of the Brillouin zone.

Conclusion: The optical excitations in NiPS3 can be consistently interpreted as dispersive singlet polarons. Similar mechanisms may govern excitonic dynamics in other layered van der Waals antiferromagnets.

Abstract: Optical and resonant inelastic X-ray scattering (RIXS) experiments in the van der Waals antiferromagnet NiPS$_3$ have revealed narrow excitonic resonances near 1.5 eV whose microscopic origin remains under debate. In this work we study the momentum dependence of these excitations using the singlet-polaron theory (SPT) previously developed for NiPS$_3$. Within its framework, we calculate the spectral function of the corresponding effective model and extract the dispersion of the resulting quasiparticles. The theory naturally predicts two singlet excitations whose dynamics arise from their propagation in the zig-zag antiferromagnetic background ($S=1$) coupled to two-magnon processes. We show that the dispersion of the lowest-energy quasiparticle agrees very well with the excitonic dispersion measured by RIXS, reproducing both its overall shape and bandwidth along high-symmetry directions of the Brillouin zone. Our results provide a consistent interpretation of the optical excitations in terms of dispersive singlet polarons, and suggest that similar mechanisms may govern excitonic dynamics in other layered van der Waals antiferromagnets.

</details>


### [120] [Altermagnetic Ground States on the Ammann--Beenker Tiling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18441)
*SeungBeom Hong,YunHo Kim,Moon Jip Park,GiBaik Sim*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper identifies exact altermagnetic ground states of a classical Heisenberg model on a quasicrystal (Ammann-Beenker tiling), derives them via the parity structure of the parent 4D hypercubic lattice, and confirms altermagnetic order through g-wave spin-resolved transport upon coupling to itinerant electrons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Altermagnets are conventionally defined by momentum-space spin splitting tied to translational symmetry, which is absent in quasicrystals. This raises the question of whether and how altermagnetic order can exist in nonperiodic systems, motivating a geometric route to altermagnetism.

Method: The authors consider a classical Heisenberg model on the Ammann-Beenker tiling and exploit the parity structure of its parent four-dimensional hypercubic lattice to determine exact ground states. They then couple the spins to itinerant electrons to probe the resulting magnetic order via transport.

Result: The ground state exhibits coplanar altermagnetic order characterized by the preserved spin-group symmetry [C2x ∥ C8z]. Despite the absence of translational symmetry (so momentum-space spin splitting is not directly accessible), coupling to itinerant electrons yields spin-resolved transport with g-wave symmetry, confirming the altermagnetic nature.

Conclusion: The work establishes a geometric route to altermagnetic order in quasicrystals, linking exact magnetic ground states to spin-resolved transport signatures and extending altermagnetism to nonperiodic systems.

Abstract: We identify exact altermagnetic ground states of a classical Heisenberg model on a quasicrystal. Considering the Ammann--Beenker tiling as an example, we determine the ground states by exploiting the parity structure of its parent four-dimensional hypercubic lattice. The ground state hosts coplanar altermagnetic order characterized by the preserved spin-group symmetry $[C_{2x}\parallel C_{8z}]$. Without translational symmetry, the momentum-space spin splitting that usually identifies altermagnets is not directly accessible. We instead couple the spins to itinerant electrons and show spin-resolved transport with $g$-wave symmetry, confirming the altermagnetic nature in a nonperiodic system. Our results establish a geometric route to altermagnetic order in quasicrystals, connecting exact magnetic ground states to their spin-resolved transport signatures.

</details>


### [121] [Matrix-product-state GPLHR for non-Hermitian many-body eigenproblems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15387)
*Xingyao Wu,Zhangyu Yang,Yanbin Chen,Yu Wang,Feng Chen*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The authors formulate the generalized preconditioned locally harmonic residual (GPLHR) method within a matrix product state (MPS) framework, using successive randomized compression (SRC), to efficiently and accurately compute multiple targeted right eigenstates of non-Hermitian many-body operators near a specified complex-plane shift.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Computing multiple eigenstates of non-Hermitian many-body operators is important for studying excitation spectra, spectral properties, and relaxation dynamics in interacting and open quantum systems, but it is challenging because eigenvectors are generally nonorthogonal and the Hilbert space grows exponentially with system size.

Method: The generalized preconditioned locally harmonic residual (GPLHR) method is formulated in a matrix product state (MPS) framework to compute multiple targeted right eigenstates with eigenvalues near a specified shift in the complex plane. Successive randomized compression (SRC) is employed to accelerate operator applications.

Result: The method is tested on transcorrelated and complex Hubbard chains, an interacting non-Hermitian Su-Schrieffer-Heeger (SSH) chain, and a Liouvillian model. GPLHR combined with MPS provides an efficient and accurate approach for computing multiple eigenstates of non-Hermitian many-body operators.

Conclusion: GPLHR combined with MPS is an efficient and accurate approach for computing multiple eigenstates of non-Hermitian many-body operators.

Abstract: Computing multiple eigenstates of non-Hermitian many-body operators plays an important role in investigating excitation spectra, spectral properties, and relaxation dynamics in interacting and open quantum systems. However, this task is challenging because the eigenvectors are generally nonorthogonal and the Hilbert space dimension grows exponentially with system size. Here, we formulate the generalized preconditioned locally harmonic residual (GPLHR) method in a matrix product state (MPS) framework to efficiently compute multiple targeted right eigenstates whose eigenvalues lie near a specified shift in the complex plane. Successive randomized compression (SRC) is employed to accelerate operator applications. We test the method on transcorrelated and complex Hubbard chains, an interacting non-Hermitian Su-Schrieffer-Heeger (SSH) chain, and a Liouvillian model. The results demonstrate that GPLHR combined with MPS provides an efficient and accurate approach for computing multiple eigenstates of non-Hermitian many-body operators.

</details>


### [122] [Dynamical chiral symmetry breaking in rotating QED$_3$: critical flavor number and the occupation-reweighting channel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15568)
*Wei Li*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Rotation does not affect dynamical chiral symmetry breaking in (2+1)D QED at zero temperature, but suppresses the chiral condensate at finite temperature, predominantly via an occupation-reweighting channel.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish how rotation impacts dynamical chiral symmetry breaking and the critical flavor number in (2+1)D QED, providing a finite-temperature reference for the model.

Method: Schwinger–Dyson (rainbow) framework on a rigidly rotating disc with ΩR<1; three operational definitions used to extract the critical flavor number; linearized rotation-response analysis decomposed into occupation-reweighting and screening channels.

Result: At T=0 the condensate's rotation response vanishes, giving Nc^SD(Ω,0)=Nc^SD(0)=2.60±0.01. At finite T, rotation suppresses the condensate: occupation-reweighting coefficient κ̂_occ=−1.55 accounts for ≥99.995% of the linearized response, amplified by 26.5 via self-consistent feedback; screening channel κ̂_scr(ε=0.01)=−0.97 with logarithmic ε→0 divergence. Crossover flavor N*(Ω) falls monotonically at fixed T.

Conclusion: Rotation leaves the T=0 critical flavor number unchanged (theorem) but suppresses the condensate at finite temperature; these results set the finite-temperature reference for the model.

Abstract: We study dynamical chiral symmetry breaking in $(2{+}1)$-dimensional QED on a rigidly rotating disc, $ΩR<1$, within the rainbow Schwinger--Dyson framework. At zero temperature the rotation response of the condensate vanishes identically, so $N_c^{\rm SD}(Ω,T{=}0)=N_c^{\rm SD}(0)=2.60\pm0.01$ (using three operational definitions). At finite temperature, rotation suppresses the condensate through a dominant occupation-reweighting channel, $\hatκ_{\rm occ}=-1.55$, accounting for $\ge 99.995\%$ of the linearized total response at the reference parameters, with self-consistent feedback amplifying it by $26.5$. The screening channel is reported at fixed regularization, $\hatκ_{\rm scr}(\varepsilon = 0.01)=-0.97$; its $\varepsilon \to 0$ behavior is logarithmically divergent. The crossover flavor $N^{*}(Ω)$ falls monotonically at fixed temperature (a corollary). The critical flavor number is unchanged by rotation at $T=0$ (theorem). Together these results set the finite-temperature reference for the model.

</details>


### [123] [Superexchange coupling between a dilute Gd3+ probe and orphan spins in a valence-bond glass](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18722)
*Patrick Colgan,Artur Solodovnyk,Jeremy P. Carlo,Scott Dietrich*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: EPR of a dilute Gd³⁺ probe in Ba₂YMoO₆ versus nonmagnetic Ba₂YNbO₆ reveals that the excess linewidth and the collapse of the half-field satellite quantify the coupling and dynamics of valence-bond-glass orphan spins.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The orphan spins in the valence-bond-glass state of Ba₂YMoO₆ are only weakly coupled and hard to access with bulk probes, so a local, site-selective probe of their coupling and dynamics is needed.

Method: Broadband coplanar-waveguide EPR at 5 K on a dilute Gd³⁺ probe substituted on the Y³⁺ site, using isostructural nonmagnetic Ba₂YNbO₆ doped identically as a fixed-lattice control; linewidth, half-field (Δm_S = ±2) satellite, and g-factor are compared between host and control.

Result: The Gd³⁺ linewidth nearly doubles in the magnetic host. Dipolar coupling to the sparse orphans is nearly an order of magnitude too weak to explain this, whereas a transferred f-d superexchange of J ~ 0.2 K reproduces the excess, making the width a static measure of orphan-spin coupling. The half-field satellite collapses in the magnetic host, evidencing a dynamical dephasing channel, while the unchanged g-factor indicates a zero-mean random internal field.

Conclusion: The static (linewidth) and dynamical (half-field collapse) EPR channels together probe both the coupling strength and the spin dynamics of valence-bond-glass orphan spins.

Abstract: The valence-bond-glass state of the 4d^1 double perovskite Ba2YMoO6 combines Mo^5+ singlets with residual paramagnetic "orphan" spins whose coupling is hard to reach with bulk probes. We probe them by electron paramagnetic resonance of a dilute Gd^3+ ion on the Y^3+ site via broadband coplanar-waveguide transmission at 5 K. The isostructural nonmagnetic niobate Ba2YNbO6, doped with the same Gd^3+ probe, is a fixed-lattice control. The Gd^3+ linewidth nearly doubles in the magnetic host. Dipolar coupling to the sparse orphans is nearly an order of magnitude too weak; the excess is instead reproduced by a transferred f-d superexchange of J ~ 0.2 K, so the width is a static measure of orphan-spin coupling. The half-field (Delta m_S = +/-2) satellite collapses in the magnetic host, a dynamical dephasing channel, while the unchanged g-factor marks a zero-mean random internal field. Together the two channels probe the coupling and dynamics of valence-bond-glass orphan spins.

</details>


### [124] [Realistic Simulation of Valence-band Photoemission Spectra by using First-principles Calculations on the Basis of FLAPW Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15602)
*Noriaki Hamada,Satoshi Nakamura,Kenta Takahashi,Mario Okawa,Tomohiko Saitoh*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The authors develop a method to compute atomic-subshell photoelectron differential cross sections (PE-DCS) for valence bands, combining partial density of states with photoionization differential cross sections, validated on Na, NaCl, and CuAlO2 hard x-ray PES data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reproducing experimental photoemission spectroscopy (PES) data requires band structure calculations that can adequately model photoelectron cross sections. Conventional muffin-tin sphere (MTS) partial density of states (PDOS) is insufficient for this purpose, and existing codes could not treat unoccupied electron shells of isolated atoms up to ~10 keV.

Method: PE-DCS of the valence band was calculated as the sum of products of PDOS and photoionization differential cross section (PI-DCS) for each atomic subshell, within a linearized augmented-plane-wave band structure framework. A "nearly-neutral atomic sphere" was proposed as a natural extension of the MTS to construct appropriate PDOS. PI-DCS was computed via the standard Hartree-Fock-Slater method, with a new computer code handling unoccupied electron shells of ground-state isolated atoms up to ~10 keV.

Result: The method was tested on two simple cases, Na metal and NaCl, and then applied to hard x-ray PES (HX-PES) data of CuAlO2. Experimental spectra were satisfactorily reproduced.

Conclusion: The proposed PE-DCS calculation approach demonstrates potential for reproducing and interpreting HX-PES spectra of many compounds.

Abstract: In order to reproduce experimental photoemission spectroscopy (PES) data by using band structure calculations with linearized augmented-plane-wave method, we developed a new method to calculate the atomic-subshell photoelectron differential cross section (PE-DCS) of the valence band. PE-DCS was calculated to be the sum of the products of the partial density of states (PDOS) and the photoionization differential cross section (PI-DCS) of each atomic subshell. To construct an appropriate PDOS beyond the conventional muffin-tin sphere (MTS) PDOS, we proposed a ``nearly-neutral atomic sphere", which can be defined as a natural extension of MTS. Although the calculational method for atomic-subshell PI-DCS was the standard Hartree-Fock-Slater method, we developed a new computer code in order to deal with the unoccupied electron shells in the ground state of isolated atoms up to $\sim$10 keV. Details of the PE-DCS calculations were examined by using two simple examples of Na metal and NaCl, and then, the method was applied to hard x-ray PES (HX-PES) data of CuAlO$_2$. The experimental spectra were satisfactorily reproduced, demonstrating the potential applications of our method to HX-PES spectra of many compounds.

</details>


### [125] [Single-Particle Spectroscopy and Cross-Barrier Dynamics in Inhomogeneous Luttinger Liquids via Unified Field Bosonization Technique](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15608)
*Soundarya P,Venkata Suryanarayana M,Joy Prakash Das*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: UFBT is used to study single-particle properties of inhomogeneous Luttinger liquids with localized impurity clusters and forward-scattering interactions, yielding power-law DDOS and tunneling conductance behaviors governed solely by interaction parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To describe local spectral properties and cross-barrier transport in inhomogeneous Luttinger liquids containing impurity clusters, without relying on perturbative expansions in impurity or interaction strength or an explicit renormalization-group treatment.

Method: The Unified Field Bosonization Technique (UFBT) is employed, with fermionic two-point correlation functions used to derive leading-order singular contributions to the dynamical density of states (DDOS) and the cross-barrier tunneling conductance.

Result: Distinct power-law behaviors of the DDOS appear near the impurity cluster and in the far-field regime. Tunneling conductance shows power-law temperature dependence: decreasing with decreasing temperature for repulsive interactions, increasing for attractive interactions, and temperature-independent in the noninteracting limit. The impurity cluster modifies amplitudes and spatial structure, while power-law exponents are fixed solely by interaction parameters.

Conclusion: UFBT provides a unified framework for describing local spectral properties and cross-barrier transport in inhomogeneous Luttinger liquids, accommodating arbitrary impurity strengths with scaling behavior emerging directly from fermionic correlation functions.

Abstract: The Unified Field Bosonization Technique (UFBT) is employed to investigate the single-particle properties of inhomogeneous Luttinger liquids containing localized clusters of impurities in the presence of forward-scattering interactions. Within this framework, fermionic two-point correlation functions are used to derive the leading-order singular contributions to the dynamical density of states (DDOS) and the cross-barrier tunneling conductance. The results reveal distinct power-law behaviours of the DDOS near the impurity cluster and in the far-field regime. The tunneling conductance exhibits a power-law temperature dependence, decreasing with decreasing temperature for repulsive interactions and increasing for attractive interactions, while remaining temperature-independent in the noninteracting limit. The impurity cluster modifies the amplitudes and spatial structure of these observables, whereas their characteristic power-law exponents are determined solely by the interaction parameters. The formulation accommodates arbitrary impurity strengths without requiring perturbative expansions in the impurity or interaction strengths. The characteristic scaling behaviour emerges directly from the fermionic correlation functions, without an explicit renormalization-group (RG) treatment of the impurity strength, while the weak-interaction limit recovers the leading perturbative correction to the tunneling response. These findings establish UFBT as a unified framework for describing local spectral properties and cross-barrier transport in inhomogeneous Luttinger liquids.

</details>


### [126] [Field Response of the Orbital Moment Probed by Integrated Magnetic Circular Dichroism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15684)
*Qian-Sheng Shi,Ziqiang Wang,Jian Kang,Yi Zhang*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This work defines a gauge-invariant MCD susceptibility for multiband systems, distinguishing it from thermodynamic orbital susceptibility. For insulators it derives contributions from quantum metric, Berry curvature, and interband mixing; for metals it separates Fermi sea and surface terms. In twisted bilayer graphene, MCD susceptibility vanishes in the charge neutrality gap while orbital susceptibility stays diamagnetic, establishing MCD susceptibility as a distinct orbital magnetic probe.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Magnetic circular dichroism is an optical probe of orbital time reversal symmetry breaking, and although its frequency-integrated form relates to the self-rotation contribution to orbital magnetization at zero field, its magnetic response generally differs from the thermodynamic orbital susceptibility. The paper aims to clarify and formalize this distinction, providing a rigorous framework for the MCD susceptibility as an independent probe of orbital magnetic response.

Method: The authors define the MCD susceptibility and derive general expressions for multiband systems using an electromagnetic U(1) gauge invariant approach. For insulators, the response is decomposed into contributions from the non-Abelian quantum metric, Berry curvature, and field-induced interband mixing. For metals, a finite-temperature Green function formulation separates the smooth response into Fermi sea and Fermi surface contributions. An independent Landau level calculation is used to verify the zero-field MCD moment and its linear magnetic response. The framework is applied to a continuum model of twisted bilayer graphene.

Result: The Landau level calculation confirms the zero-field MCD moment and its linear magnetic response. In twisted bilayer graphene, the MCD susceptibility vanishes within the charge neutrality gap, whereas the thermodynamic orbital susceptibility remains markedly diamagnetic. Near the gap edges, the orbital moment term dominates the response, and features near the van Hove energies are associated with density of states enhancement.

Conclusion: The two susceptibilities reflect different orbital processes even for the same electronic structure, establishing the MCD susceptibility as a distinct and complementary probe of orbital magnetic response relative to the thermodynamic orbital susceptibility.

Abstract: Magnetic circular dichroism (MCD) is an optical probe of orbital time reversal symmetry breaking. Although frequency integrated MCD is related to the self-rotation contribution to orbital magnetization at zero field, its magnetic response generally differs from the thermodynamic orbital susceptibility. Here, we define the MCD susceptibility and derive general expressions for multiband systems using an electromagnetic $U(1)$ gauge invariant approach. For insulators, the response contains contributions from the non-Abelian quantum metric, Berry curvature, and field induced interband mixing. For metals, a finite temperature Green function formulation separates the smooth response into Fermi sea and Fermi surface contributions. An independent Landau level calculation confirms the expressions for the zero-field MCD moment and its linear magnetic response. In a continuum model for twisted bilayer graphene, the MCD susceptibility vanishes within the charge neutrality gap, while the thermodynamic orbital susceptibility remains markedly diamagnetic. Near the gap edges, the orbital moment term dominates the response. Features near the van Hove energies are associated with density of states enhancement. These results clarify how the two susceptibilities reflect different orbital processes even for the same electronic structure and thus establish the MCD susceptibility as a distinct probe of orbital magnetic response.

</details>


### [127] [Magpie: A Graphical Magnon Software](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15790)
*Tobias Weber*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Magpie is an open-source, fully graphical linear spin-wave software written in portable C++, focused on 3D visualization of magnetic models and magnon dispersion relations, featuring a modular architecture and code generators for interoperability with other spin-wave libraries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing linear spin-wave tools often lack intuitive 3D graphical visualization of magnetic models and magnon dispersions, and are difficult to extend or integrate with other libraries. Magpie addresses the need for a portable, extensible, and visually oriented spin-wave computation environment.

Method: Magpie is implemented in high-performance, portable C++ under an open-source license. It employs a modular architecture enabling rapid development of new calculation modules and inclusion into external applications. Integrated code generators provide interoperability with other linear spin-wave libraries, and a fully graphical interface drives 3D visualization of magnetic models and magnon dispersion relations.

Result: The software delivers 3D visualization of magnetic models and magnon dispersion relations, supports modular extension for new calculation modules, and achieves seamless interoperability with external linear spin-wave libraries via its code generators.

Conclusion: Magpie offers a portable, open-source, graphically oriented platform for linear spin-wave calculations, combining modular extensibility and cross-library interoperability, thereby facilitating rapid development and integration of spin-wave simulation tools.

Abstract: Magpie is a fully graphical linear spin-wave software which has its main focus on 3-d visualisation of magnetic models and magnon dispersion relations. The software is distributed under an open-source license and is written in high-performance, portable C++. Its modular architecture allows for a rapid development of new calculation modules and permits the inclusion into external applications. Included code generators ensure seamless interoperability with other linear spin-wave libraries.

</details>


### [128] [Variational Minimization of Almost Strong Zero Modes in Parafermionic Wires](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15748)
*Niklas Tausendpfund,Matteo Rizzi*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper introduces a quantum-inspired variational algorithm to search for almost strong zero modes (ASZMs) in generic parafermionic chains, reproducing exact results and finding strongly bound parafermionic operators in non-integrable models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Strongly interacting fermionic chains with Z_n symmetry host topological phases with degenerate ground-state manifolds connected by parafermionic zero modes. Although exactly preserved at zero temperature, interactions destabilize these modes at finite energy densities, and obtaining their lifetime in generic Hamiltonians is often impossible because it requires the full spectrum or long time evolutions.

Method: The authors propose a novel quantum-inspired algorithm that variationally searches for almost strong zero modes in generic parafermionic chain models.

Result: Analytical and numerical demonstrations show that the ansatz faithfully reproduces exact results and finds strongly bound parafermionic operators in the non-integrable Kitaev-Hubbard chain and the chiral Potts model.

Conclusion: The results allow verification of the existence of a true strong zero mode in the symmetric limit of the chiral Potts model for Z_3-parafermions.

Abstract: Strongly interacting fermionic chains obeying a $Z_n$ symmetry can host topological phases with highly degenerate ground-state manifolds. The different ground states are connected by strongly bound fractionalized edge modes called parafermionic zero modes. While exactly preserved under time evolution at zero temperature, interactions lead to a destabilization of these modes at finite energy densities, and, given a generic Hamiltonian, obtaining the lifetime of these modes is often impossible as it requires knowledge of the full spectrum of the Hamiltonian or long time evolutions. Here, we propose a novel quantum-inspired algorithm that can be used to variationally search for these almost strong zero modes in generic parafermionic chain models. We demonstrate analytically and numerically that our ansatz faithfully reproduces the exact results and finds strongly bound parafermionic operators in the non-integrable Kitaev-Hubbard chain and chiral Potts model. Our results further allow us to verify the existence of a true strong zero mode in the symmetrical limit of the chiral Potts model for $Z_3$-parafermions.

</details>


### [129] [Electronic Structure of a Delafossite Oxide CuAlO$_2$ in Comparison with CuCrO$_2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16061)
*Kenta Takahashi,Ryo Kato,Mario Okawa,Tetsuji Okuda,Akira Yasui,Eiji Ikenaga,Kanta Ono,Noriaki Hamada,Tomohiko Saitoh*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The Cu valence in CuAlO₂ is monovalent (unlike CuCrO₂), verified by resonant and core-level photoemission together with LDA+U calculations; a charge-transfer model involving empty Cr 3d orbitals explains the delafossite electronic structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Contradictory reports on the Cu valence in copper delafossites motivate a systematic comparison between CuAlO₂ and CuCrO₂ to clarify how empty 3d orbitals govern the electronic structure.

Method: Vacuum ultraviolet and hard x-ray photoemission spectroscopy (including Cu 3p–3d resonant PES and Cu 2p core-level HX-PES) combined with local-density approximation + U band structure calculations.

Result: Cu 3p–3d resonant PES and Cu 2p HX-PES show that Cu in CuAlO₂ is monovalent, contrasting with the method-dependent behavior of CuCrO₂. A weak Cu²⁺ satellite without a distinct main peak appears in the Cu 2p HX-PES of CuCrO₂. Valence-band HX-PES and calculations reveal significant Cu 4s and Al 3s/Cr 4s character in the valence band.

Conclusion: The observations are consistently described by the previously proposed Cu 3d,4s–Cr 3d charge-transfer model, demonstrating the critical role of empty 3d orbitals in the electronic structure of copper delafossite oxides.

Abstract: We investigated the electronic structure of a delafossite oxide CuAlO$_2$ in comparison with CuCrO$_2$, using vacuum ultraviolet (VUV) and hard x-ray (HX) photoemission spectroscopy (PES) and band structure calculations within the local-density approximation +$U$ scheme. Cu 3$p\! -\! 3d$ resonant PES and Cu $2p$ core-level HX-PES give concrete evidence that the Cu valence in CuAlO$_2$ is monovalent, which makes a sharp contrast to CuCrO$_2$ that showed contradictory results depending upon probing methods [Yokobori \textit{et al.}, Phys. Rev. B \textbf{87}, 195124 (2013)]. We further noticed weak but detectable satellite structures due to Cu$^{2+}$ state in the Cu $2p$ HX-PES spectrum of CuCrO$_2$, surprisingly, without an observable main peak due to the Cu$^{2+}$ state. Comparisons between the valence-band HX-PES spectra and the band structure calculations revealed a significant amount of the Cu $4s$ and Al $3s$/Cr $4s$ states in the valence band. The above results can reasonably be explained by the Cu $3d, 4s-$Cr $3d$ charge transfer model that we previously proposed in the above paper, thereby demonstrating the critical role of empty $3d$ orbitals in the electronic structure of the copper delafossite oxides.

</details>


### [130] [Honeycomb Crystal in a Mass-Asymmetric Electron-Hole Bilayer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17030)
*Shengdu Chai,Wangqian Miao,Yuqiang Li,Lei Wang,Xi Dai,X. C. Xie*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Correlated mobile electrons in a mass-asymmetric electron-hole bilayer can stabilize a honeycomb electron-hole crystal (rather than the usual triangular Wigner crystal), which emerges at a density-driven transition and is experimentally feasible in a BLG/hBN/WS$_2$-WSe$_2$ heterostructure at $n\sim10^{11}~\text{cm}^{-2}$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: A two-dimensional electron gas on a uniform background forms a triangular Wigner crystal at low density. In a strongly mass-asymmetric electron-hole bilayer, localized heavy holes provide a nonuniform positive background that supports an electron crystal at densities two orders of magnitude higher than for a homogeneous electron gas in the same material. The paper asks whether correlated mobile electrons can overturn the preference for triangular order and instead stabilize a honeycomb electron-hole crystal.

Method: Real-space neural-network variational Monte Carlo (NN-VMC) in the Born-Oppenheimer limit with fixed holes. Spin states are analyzed on the honeycomb hole lattice, and the results are mapped to the half-filled Hubbard model; complex polarization is used to probe the metal-insulator transition.

Result: Increasing density drives a transition from a triangular electron-hole crystal to a honeycomb crystal, which eventually melts into itinerant electrons coexisting with the honeycomb or triangular hole lattice at fixed interlayer separation. Honeycomb order emerges when gains in electronic kinetic energy and electron-hole attraction outweigh its higher intralayer Coulomb repulsion, a threshold never reached in the rigid point-dipole model. The honeycomb hole lattice supports paramagnetic, antiferromagnetic, and stripe-ordered spin states. Complex polarization indicates a metal-insulator transition, with states consistent with a stripe-ordered Dirac semimetal and paramagnetic or antiferromagnetic Mott insulators via mapping to the half-filled Hubbard model.

Conclusion: Correlated mobile electrons can overturn the preference for triangular order and stabilize a honeycomb electron-hole crystal. The authors propose a BLG/hBN/WS$_2$-WSe$_2$ heterostructure as a feasible platform for the predicted honeycomb crystal, at an experimentally accessible density $n\sim10^{11}~\text{cm}^{-2}$.

Abstract: A two-dimensional electron gas in a uniform neutralizing background forms a triangular Wigner crystal at low density. In a strongly mass-asymmetric electron-hole bilayer, localized heavy holes instead provide a nonuniform positive background that supports an electron crystal at densities two orders of magnitude higher than for a homogeneous electron gas in the same material. Here we show that correlated mobile electrons can overturn the preference for triangular order, stabilizing a honeycomb electron-hole crystal. Using the powerful real-space neural-network variational Monte Carlo in the Born-Oppenheimer limit with fixed holes, we find that increasing the density drives a transition from a triangular electron-hole crystal to a honeycomb crystal, which eventually melts into itinerant electrons coexisting with the honeycomb or the triangular hole lattice at fixed interlayer separation. Relative to triangular order, honeycomb order emerges when gains in electronic kinetic energy and electron-hole attraction outweigh its higher intralayer Coulomb repulsion, a threshold never reached in the rigid point-dipole model. Moreover, the honeycomb hole lattice supports paramagnetic, antiferromagnetic, and stripe-ordered spin states. Complex polarization indicates a metal-insulator transition, while the states on the honeycomb hole lattice are consistent with a stripe-ordered Dirac semimetal and paramagnetic or antiferromagnetic Mott insulators via mapping to the half-filled Hubbard model. We further propose a BLG/hBN/WS$_2$-WSe$_2$ heterostructure as a platform for the predicted honeycomb crystal, at an experimentally feasible density $n\sim10^{11}~\text{cm}^{-2}$.

</details>


### [131] [Field-driven phase transition crossover in kagome antiferromagnet Mn$_{3-x}$Co$_x$Sn](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16189)
*Shubhankar Paul,Chanchal Sow*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Co doping at the Mn site of Mn$_3$Sn breaks kagome lattice symmetry and tunes the magnetic phase transition order, as probed via the magnetocaloric effect in Mn$_{3-x}$Co$_x$Sn single crystals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Mn$_3$Sn has a complex magnetic phase diagram with multiple symmetry-breaking phenomena, and minute electron doping at the Mn site is known to break the kagome lattice symmetry. The authors exploit this doping to tune the order of the magnetic phase transition.

Method: Magnetocaloric effect measurements on Mn$_{3-x}$Co$_x$Sn single crystals, complemented by scaled magnetic entropy change analysis, to track spin-reorientation and weak-ferromagnetic transitions and construct a new phase diagram.

Result: Pristine Mn$_3$Sn is metallic with a spin-reorientation transition at 278 K, shifting to 175 K upon Co doping. Co doping induces a weak-ferromagnetic transition near ~210 K and produces an effect inverse to the conventional magnetocaloric effect, with enhanced magnetic entropy change. The scaled entropy change reveals a field-driven crossover from first- to second-order magnetic phase transition in Mn$_{2.75}$Co$_{0.25}$Sn.

Conclusion: A new phase diagram is constructed from experimental data, depicting the field evolution of the various magnetic orders in Co-doped Mn$_3$Sn.

Abstract: Mn$_3$Sn exhibits a complex magnetic phase diagram with multiple symmetry-breaking phenomena. Minute electron doping at the Mn site breaks the kagome lattice symmetry in Mn$_3$Sn. In this work, we exploited this to tune the order of the magnetic phase transition using magnetocaloric effect in Mn$_{3-x}$Co$_x$Sn single crystals. Pristine Mn$_3$Sn shows metallic behaviour with a spin-reorientation transition at 278 K, which shifts to 175 K upon Co doping. Additionally, Co doping also induces a weak-ferromagnetic transition near $\approx 210$ K. The effect of Co doping results inverse to conventional magnetocaloric effect, with enhanced magnetic entropy change. Further, the scaled entropy change reveals a field-driven magnetic phase transition crossover from first order to second order in Mn$_{2.75}$Co$_{0.25}$Sn crystal. Based on experimental data, a new phase diagram is constructed, depicting the field evolution of various magnetic orders.

</details>


### [132] [Thermally Enhanced Pseudo-Goldstone Spin Gap in the Isotropic Helimagnet ZnCr$_2$Se$_4$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16439)
*N. D. Andriushin,A. Sanders,J. Reuther,D. C. Peets,P. Steffens,A. Hiess,J. Lass,L. Prodan,V. Tsurkan,V. Felea,D. S. Inosov*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Inelastic neutron scattering reveals that the pseudo-Goldstone magnon gap in the helimagnet ZnCr$_2$Se$_4$ grows nonmonotonically on warming before closing near $T_\mathrm{N}$, consistent with quartic-potential spin-dynamics simulations and driven by thermal fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In magnetically ordered systems the spin gap in the magnon spectrum is normally expected to decrease on warming and close at the ordering transition; the pseudo-Goldstone gap's precise origin and behavior remain debated, motivating a direct experimental probe of its temperature dependence.

Method: Inelastic neutron scattering measurements of the magnon spectrum of the Heisenberg helimagnet ZnCr$_2$Se$_4$ across its ordered phase, compared with spin-dynamic simulations based on an effective quartic potential in an isotropic system.

Result: The pseudo-Goldstone gap exhibits an atypical, nonmonotonic temperature dependence: it increases over much of the ordered phase before closing near $T_\mathrm{N}$. The data align with the effective quartic-potential simulations. At subkelvin temperatures the gap is dominated by quantum effects, and its characteristic growth on warming is caused by thermal fluctuations.

Conclusion: The pseudo-Goldstone spin gap in ZnCr$_2$Se$_4$ shows anomalous nonmonotonic thermal evolution that contradicts the usual expectation of monotonic gap closing; this behavior is explained by thermal-fluctuation-driven effects within a quartic-potential framework, clarifying the origin of pseudo-Goldstone gaps in isotropic magnets.

Abstract: In magnetically ordered systems, the spin gap in the magnon spectrum is normally expected to decrease on warming and close at the transition out of the long-range order. The presence of a pseudo-Goldstone gap is commonly associated with nonlinear terms in spin-wave theory. However, its precise origin and detailed properties can vary and remain under debate. Here we show by means of inelastic neutron scattering that the pseudo-Goldstone gap in the Heisenberg helimagnet ZnCr$_2$Se$_4$ exhibits an atypical, nonmonotonic temperature dependence: the gap increases over much of the ordered phase before closing near $T_\mathrm{N}$. These results align with recent spin-dynamic simulations based on an effective quartic potential in an isotropic system~[Phys. Rev. B \textbf{113}, L220411 (2026)]. As the pseudo-Goldstone spin gap in ZnCr$_2$Se$_4$ is dominated by quantum effects at subkelvin temperatures, its characteristic growth on warming is caused by thermal fluctuations.

</details>


### [133] [Scalable Quantum Monte Carlo for Shannon-Rényi Entropy: An Experiment-visitable Probe of Quantum Phases and Criticality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16494)
*Dong-Xu Liu,Zhe Wang,Zenan Liu Yi-Ming Ding,Zheng Yan*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The authors develop a quantum Monte Carlo framework that computes the Shannon-Rényi entropy efficiently (polynomial cost) via replica gluing and reweight-annealing, and establish its finite-size scaling theory near quantum critical points.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Shannon-Rényi entropy is experimentally accessible from single-shot snapshots in cold-atom experiments but its theoretical evaluation in interacting systems costs exponentially. A scalable method and a rigorous scaling framework were missing.

Method: Construct a field-theoretic spacetime manifold where q replicas are glued at imaginary-time boundaries, expressing SR entropy as a ratio of generalized partition functions. Develop a reweight-annealing framework with two schemes: a virtual-parameter scheme interpolating unglued to fully glued limits via binomial-weighted partial gluing, and a real-parameter scheme annealing a physical coupling constant within stochastic series expansion.

Result: Computational cost reduced from exponential to polynomial. A finite-size scaling theory for the SR entropy near a quantum critical point is established, S_q = a(g)L^2 + S_q^0((g-g_c)L^{1/ν}); data collapse of its first derivative yields ν for O(3) and Ising universality classes (spin-1/2 columnar dimerized Heisenberg model and bilayer Heisenberg-Ising model). Simulations extract Goldstone-mode count N_G=2 and Z_2 ground-state degeneracy ln 2.

Conclusion: The SR entropy becomes a standalone, order-parameter-free diagnostic of quantum criticality with scaling status equal to entanglement entropy, closing the loop between theory and quantum simulation by enabling quantitative benchmarking of experimental snapshot data.

Abstract: The Shannon-Rényi (SR) entropy characterizes the probability distribution of many-body wave functions in a chosen basis and is directly accessible from single-shot measurement snapshots in cold-atom experiments, yet its theoretical evaluation in interacting systems carries exponential cost. Here we construct a field-theoretic spacetime manifold for the SR entropy in which $q$ replicas are glued at the imaginary-time boundaries, recasting it as a ratio of generalized partition functions amenable to large-scale quantum Monte Carlo simulation. On this basis we develop a reweight-annealing framework with two complementary strategies: a virtual-parameter scheme interpolating between unglued and fully glued limits via binomial-weighted partial gluing, and a real-parameter scheme using a physical coupling constant as the annealing variable within stochastic series expansion. Both reduce the computational cost from exponential to polynomial. Most importantly, we establish for the first time a finite-size scaling theory for the SR entropy near a quantum critical point, $S_q=a(g)L^2+S_q^0\left((g-g_c)L^{1/ν}\right)$; data collapse of its first derivative yields the correlation-length exponent $ν$ for both the O(3) and Ising universality classes, realized in the spin-$\frac{1}{2}$ columnar dimerized Heisenberg model and the bilayer Heisenberg-Ising model. The same simulations extract the Goldstone-mode count $N_G=2$ and the $Z_2$ ground-state degeneracy $\ln 2$ from subleading terms. These results elevate the SR entropy to a standalone, order-parameter-free diagnostic of quantum criticality with the same scaling status as the entanglement entropy, and close the loop between theory and quantum simulation by enabling quantitative benchmarking of experimental snapshots from state-of-the-art quantum simulators.

</details>


### [134] [Topological Chiral Superconductivity from Doping Correlated Charge Transfer Insulator with Orbital Magnetism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16674)
*Wen Sun,Yuan Yang,Shou-Shu Gong,Wen Huang*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This work establishes a real-space route to topological chiral p+ip superconductivity arising from chiral charge-transfer excitations upon doping a strongly interacting charge-transfer insulator with orbital magnetism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Topological chiral superconductivity is usually understood via momentum-space electronic structures, but its emergence from real-space processes in strongly correlated systems is largely unexplored. The authors aim to identify a microscopic, real-space mechanism for chiral superconductivity.

Method: A doped charge-transfer insulator is studied in a strongly repulsive spinless-fermion model with three sublattices and 2π flux per unit cell, using perturbative analysis, exact diagonalization, and density matrix renormalization group simulations.

Result: A topological superconducting ground state with p+ip symmetry is established. In the large charge-transfer-gap limit, chiral charge-transfer excitations generate a correlated-hopping process that drives electron pairing and its chiral structure. The chiral superconductivity persists into the smaller-gap regime, where chiral excitonic two-particle bound states can emerge.

Conclusion: The results provide a concrete real-space route to topological chiral superconductivity via doping strongly interacting charge-transfer insulators with orbital magnetism.

Abstract: Topological chiral superconductivity has been extensively studied from the perspective of electronic structures in momentum space, while its emergence from real-space processes in strongly interacting systems remains largely unexplored. Here, we study a doped charge-transfer insulator in a strongly repulsive spinless-fermion model with three sublattices and $2π$ flux per unit cell. Combining perturbative analysis, exact diagonalization, and density matrix renormalization group simulations, we establish a topological superconducting ground state with $p+ip$ symmetry. In the large charge-transfer gap limit, chiral charge-transfer excitations generate a correlated-hopping process that provides a microscopic mechanism for electron pairing and its chiral structure. The chiral superconductivity persists into the smaller-gap regime, where chiral excitonic two-particle bound state can emerge. Our results establish a concrete real-space route to topological chiral superconductivity upon doping strongly interacting charge-transfer insulators with orbital magnetism.

</details>


### [135] [Confinement-deconfinement transitions in the dimer-loop models on two-dimensional non-bipartite lattices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16676)
*Jay Pandey,Souvik Kundu,Kedar Damle*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el
