Motivation: Quasiperiodic modulation typically localizes states and destroys spatial extent; the paper asks whether a uniform orbital detuning can restore delocalization even as the modulation amplitude grows.

Method: A trimer ladder with fixed hoppings and orbital modulation signs is studied. Increasing detuning and modulation amplitude together produces successive avoided crossings that redistribute orbital weights. Band participation scaling, wave-packet propagation, Gaussian-quadrature-improved closed-walk determinant estimates, and comparison of coupled versus decoupled ladders under a finite-size participation criterion are used.

Result: Detuning suppresses the projected onsite potential in both lower molecular bands while the third remains strongly modulated, producing two reentrant high-participation windows. Wave-packet propagation confirms spatial recovery at representative controls. Residual interband modulation generates a self-energy dependent on molecular level separation. Restoring full off-diagonal modulation narrows both sampled windows, contracting the second more in absolute terms.

Conclusion: Uniform orbital detuning can restore spatial extent against strengthening quasiperiodic modulation. Diagonal cancellations leave a self-energy governed by molecular level separation, and off-diagonal modulation contraction asymmetrically narrows the participation windows.

Abstract: Uniform orbital detuning can restore spatial extent even as quasiperiodic modulation strengthens. In a trimer ladder with fixed hoppings and orbital modulation signs, increasing detuning and modulation amplitude together drives successive avoided crossings that redistribute orbital weights, suppressing the projected onsite potential in both lower molecular bands while leaving the third strongly modulated. Complete-band participation scaling supports two reentrant high-participation windows, with wave-packet propagation corroborating spatial recovery at representative controls over the studied sizes and times. These diagonal cancellations leave residual interband modulation, which generates a self-energy whose magnitude depends on the molecular level separation. Gaussian quadrature improves convergence of closed-walk determinant estimates used to cross-check energy-resolved attenuation. Comparing the coupled and decoupled ladders under the same finite-size participation criterion shows that restoring the full off-diagonal modulation narrows both sampled windows, with a larger absolute contraction of the second.

</details>


<div id='physics.app-ph'></div>

# physics.app-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [250] [Optimization of Active Reconfigurable Surfaces for Spatiotemporal MU-MIMO Wave Routing in Disordered Media: Application to the Cocktail-Party Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14328)
*Frederic J. N. Jorgensen,Florent Dorlot,Constant Bourdeloux,Mathias Fink,Fabrice Lemoult,Steven G. Johnson,Raphaël Pestourie*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: This paper designs an active reconfigurable intelligent surface (RIS) for acoustic multi-user MIMO communication by solving a large-scale convex optimization problem, achieving broadband spatial-temporal focusing with suppressed crosstalk.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Acoustic multi-user MIMO communication suffers from crosstalk between users and requires precise spatial and temporal focusing of broadband signals. Existing time-reversal methods are insufficient in crosstalk suppression and temporal focusing.

Method: Formulate and solve a large-scale convex optimization problem to design an active RIS, enabling each emitter's broadband signal to focus in space and time at its intended receivers while suppressing crosstalk.

Result: In acoustic experiments, the proposed approach outperforms existing time-reversal methods by 5.1-7.6 dB in crosstalk suppression while achieving better temporal focusing.

Conclusion: The active RIS design via convex optimization is effective for acoustic MU-MIMO, providing superior crosstalk suppression and temporal focusing compared to time-reversal methods.

Abstract: We design an active reconfigurable intelligent surface (RIS) for acoustic multi-user multiple-input multiple-output (MU-MIMO) communication by solving a large-scale convex optimization problem, so that each emitter's broadband signal focuses in space and time at its intended receivers while crosstalk is suppressed. In acoustic experiments, we show that our approach outperforms existing time-reversal methods by 5.1-7.6 dB in crosstalk suppression, while achieving better temporal focusing.

</details>


### [251] [UV-cured PVA-collagen co-network with staining-free photodynamic activity against chronic wound bacteria](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14732)
*Nikitia Mexia,Charles Brooker,Giuseppe Tronci*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: A UV-cured PVA/collagen co-network material with work-light-triggered photodynamic antibacterial activity, high swelling, tunable wet-state mechanics, and no staining is developed for chronic wound healing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Chronic wounds affect 1-2% of the global population and pose an unsustainable healthcare and economic burden. Delayed healing is mainly driven by bacterial infection and inadequate moisture balance, which are difficult to manage due to rising antimicrobial resistance and the limited exudate management and mechanical performance of current dressings.

Method: A staining-free photodynamic material was prepared by UV co-curing an aqueous solution of polyvinyl alcohol functionalised with phenothiazine (toluidine blue) and methacrylate residues, 4-vinylbenzyl-functionalised type 1 rat tail collagen, and a water-soluble photoinitiator. Characterisation included FTIR and DSC of the co-networks, 96-hour in vitro incubation (pH 7.4, 37 degC) to assess dye retention, medium colouration, and mass retention, and work-light exposure (10 min) to evaluate antibacterial efficacy against Staphylococcus aureus and Pseudomonas aeruginosa versus dark controls, plus L929 fibroblast metabolic activity.

Result: The material exhibited work-light-triggered antibacterial activity, water-induced swelling of more than nine-fold increase in dry weight, and tunable wet-state compression modulus (Ec: 76+/-34 - 289+/-94 kPa). FTIR and DSC confirmed characteristic chemical and thermal features of both components. Incubation showed >93 wt.% toluidine blue retention, suppressed medium colouration, and >98 wt.% average mass retention. Work-light exposure gave at least 1 log10 reduction in S. aureus and P. aeruginosa viability versus dark controls, while L929 fibroblast metabolic activity remained above 90%.

Conclusion: The combination of antibacterial properties, staining-free photodynamic behaviour, water-induced swelling, and wet-state mechanical properties achieved via network integration with functionalised collagen makes this material appealing for chronic wound healing and on-demand delivery of localised therapies.

Abstract: Chronic wounds affect 1-2% of the global population and represent an unsustainable healthcare, financial and economic burden. Delayed healing is largely associated with bacterial infection and inadequate moisture balance, both of which remain difficult to control due to increasing antimicrobial resistance and the limited exudate management capacity and mechanical performance of current dressings. To address these challenges, we introduce a staining-free photodynamic material with work-light-triggered antibacterial activity, significant water-induced swelling (more than nine-fold increase in dry weight) and tunable wet state compression modulus (Ec: 76+/-34 - 289+/-94 kPa). The material was prepared by UV co-curing of an aqueous solution containing polyvinyl alcohol functionalised with both phenothiazine and methacrylate residues, 4-vinylbenzyl-functionalised type 1 rat tail collagen, and a water-soluble photoinitiator. Fourier Transform Infrared spectroscopy and Differential Scanning Calorimetry of the co-networks indicated the presence of characteristic chemical and thermal features of both components. 96-hour co-network incubation in vitro (pH 7.4, 37 degC) confirmed the significant retention of toluidine blue (> 93 wt.%), suppressed medium colouration, and an average mass retention higher than 98 wt.%. Exposure of the UV-cured samples to 10-min work light resulted in at least 1 log10 reduction in Staphylococcus aureus and Pseudomonas aeruginosa viabilities, in contrast to dark-control conditions, while the metabolic activity of L929 fibroblasts remained above 90%. The antibacterial properties, staining-free photodynamic behaviour, water-induced swelling, and wet-state mechanical properties achieved via network integration with functionalised collagen, make this material appealing for chronic wound healing applications and for on-demand delivery of localised therapies.

</details>


### [252] [Sub-Micron Gate $β-Ga_{2}O_{3}$ FinFETs with Current Density $>$ 750 mA/mm and Ion/Ioff Ratio $>$ $10^{9}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15155)
*Nabasindhu Das,Cameron Gorsak,Pushpanshu Tripathi,Ankita Kashyap,Noor Jahan Nipu,Advait Gilankar,Hari Nair,Uttam Singisetti,Trevor Thornton,Nidhin Kurian Kalarickal*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: Submicron-gate β-Ga₂O₃ FinFETs combining Si delta-doped channels with selectively regrown n++ source/drain achieve high drive current, strong current modulation, and GHz operation, showing promise for power and RF electronics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: β-Ga₂O₃ has a high critical field and wide bandgap that make it attractive for power and RF electronics, but obtaining simultaneously high drive current, strong electrostatic control (current modulation), and GHz-frequency operation in a scalable device platform remains a key challenge.

Method: The device integrates Si delta-doped channels with selectively regrown n++ source/drain contacts. It uses 100 nm-wide fins for improved electrostatic control of a high sheet charge density channel (3.3×10¹³ cm⁻²), with a 150 nm gate length; characterization includes DC I-V, transconductance, de-embedded fT/fMAX, and pulsed I-V measurements.

Result: The 150 nm-gate FinFET shows a maximum drain current density of 764 mA/mm (VGS = 1 V, VDS = 10 V), on-resistance of 9.1 Ω·mm, Ion/Ioff above 10⁹, peak transconductance of 43 mS/mm, and de-embedded fT/fMAX of 6.3/2.0 GHz. Pulsed I-V shows ~20% current collapse, attributed mainly to trapping at etched fin sidewalls and the Al₂O₃/β-Ga₂O₃ interface.

Conclusion: Delta-doped β-Ga₂O₃ FinFETs can simultaneously achieve high drive current, strong current modulation, and GHz-frequency operation, offering a scalable device platform for future power and RF electronics.

Abstract: In this work, we demonstrate submicron-gate $β-Ga_{2}O_{3}$ FinFETs that integrate Si delta-doped channels with selectively regrown n++ source/drain contacts. The devices employ 100 nm-wide fins to improve electrostatic control of a high sheet charge density channel of $3.3\times 10^{13}$ cm-2. A 150 nm-gate-length FinFET exhibits a maximum drain current density of 764 mA/mm at VGS = 1 V and VDS = 10 V, an on-resistance of 9.1 $Ω$.mm, and an Ion/Ioff ratio exceeding $10^9$. The device also shows a peak transconductance of 43 mS/mm and de-embedded $f_{T}/f_{MAX}$ values of 6.3/2.0 GHz. Pulsed I-V measurements indicate ~20 % current collapse, attributed primarily to trapping at etched fin sidewalls and the $Al_{2} O_{3}$/ $β-Ga_{2}O_{3}$ interface. These results show that delta-doped $β-Ga_{2}O_{3}$ FinFETs can simultaneously achieve high drive current, strong current modulation, and GHz-frequency operation, providing a scalable device platform for future power and RF electronics.

</details>


### [253] [Directivity Estimates and Tolerance Tradeoffs in the Presence of Random Perturbations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15192)
*Kurt Schab,Lukas Jelinek,Miloslav Capek,Mats Gustafsson*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: This work develops generalized sensitivity metrics to predict how perturbations reduce the directivity of high-directivity sources, applicable to continuous sources, ideal uncoupled arrays, and heterogeneous arrays with mutual coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Realizing high-directivity sources in practice is challenging because perturbations degrade achievable directivity. Prior tolerance studies were limited to arrays; a generalized framework was needed to predict directivity reduction across diverse source types.

Method: Sensitivity metrics are formulated using the error model from previous array-tolerance studies but extended to heterogeneous array characteristics in an arbitrary source-expansion basis. The formulation yields the ensemble average of realized directivity under the chosen error model, acting as a fast predictor of directivity reduction. Required data are obtained via simulation or measurement, with simplification for lossless systems.

Result: The metrics enable optimization problems capturing the tradeoff between directivity and tolerance. Examples illustrate these tradeoffs, special cases, comparisons with prior maximum-directivity estimates, and the accuracy of sensitivity as a predictor of statistical performance over large ensembles.

Conclusion: The proposed generalized sensitivity measures provide a practical, accurate tool for predicting and optimizing the realized directivity of high-directivity sources under perturbation.

Abstract: The practicality of realizing high-directivity sources is studied using a generalized sensitivity metrics. These measures are based on the error model adopted in previous studies on array tolerance, but account for heterogeneous array characteristics in an arbitrary source-expansion basis, making it applicable to the study of continuous sources, ideal uncoupled arrays, and heterogeneous arrays with mutual coupling. The formulation provides the ensemble average of realized directivity under the selected error model, making it a fast predictor of anticipated directivity reduction due to source perturbation. The data required to compute these predictions are straightforward to obtain via simulation or measurement, with a significant simplification when the system can be approximated as lossless. The directivity and the proposed tolerance measures allow for the formulation of optimization problems illustrating the tradeoff between these measures. Several examples demonstrate these tradeoffs, special cases, comparison to previous estimates of maximum directivity, and the accuracy of sensitivity as a predictor of statistical performance over large ensembles of design realizations.

</details>


### [254] [Acoustic Scattered Field Calculation for a Moving Source Using Time-Dependent Physical Optics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15823)
*Asaf Kor,Gal Ben Yelid,Amir Boag,Lea Beilkin-Sirota*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a time-dependent physical optics (TDPO) formulation to compute the acoustic scattered field of a stationary object illuminated by a moving source in 3D, valid in the near field, for arbitrary trajectories and bandwidths, supplemented by a Fock correction near/beyond the shadow boundary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: A moving source (potentially fast) produces a complex wave field beyond a simple Doppler effect, especially in the near-field regime, which the conventional frequency-domain physical optics (PO) method cannot handle properly. There is a need for a method that explicitly accounts for source motion while remaining computationally efficient compared to full numerical simulations.

Method: The authors formulate a time-domain variant of physical optics (TDPO) that explicitly accounts for source motion, rather than using frequency-domain PO. The algorithm supports arbitrary source trajectories, arbitrary signal bandwidth, and high source velocities relative to the speed of sound. Near and beyond the shadow boundary, where PO is least accurate, a Fock correction of the surface field—extended to a moving source with a harmonic signal—is added.

Result: The method matches or exceeds the accuracy of finite element simulations while reducing computational cost by orders of magnitude.

Conclusion: The proposed TDPO formulation with Fock correction provides an accurate and highly efficient approach for computing acoustic scattered fields from a stationary object under a moving source, covering near-field and shadow-boundary regimes, arbitrary trajectories and bandwidths, and high source velocities.

Abstract: We present a time-dependent physical optics (TDPO) formulation for computing the acoustic scattered field of a stationary object in response to a moving source in three-dimensional space. Due to the motion of the source, which may be fast, the conventional frequency-domain PO method is not employed. Instead, a time-domain variant that explicitly accounts for the motion is formulated. The study focuses on the near-field regime, where source motion induces a complex wave field beyond a simple Doppler effect. The proposed algorithm accommodates arbitrary source trajectories and arbitrary signal bandwidth, as well as high source velocities relative to the speed of sound. Near and beyond the shadow boundary, where PO is least accurate, the formulation is supplemented by a Fock correction of the surface field, extended here to a moving source with a harmonic signal. The method matches or exceeds the accuracy of finite element simulations at a computational cost lower by orders of magnitude.

</details>


### [255] [Wireless Energy Transfer for Space Applications: A Review of Technologies, Mission Architectures, Energy Routing, and System Integration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16960)
*Lyuye Lin,Zhifu Feng,Ermanno Miele,Quanbo Ge,Manuel Iori,Chunbo Liu,Kwok-Tong Chau,Denis Garoli,Remo Proietti Zaccaria*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: This review organizes wireless energy transfer (WET) for space applications along four axes—physical mechanism, energy reference plane, mission architecture, and evidence maturity—using a unified R0–R7 energy-accounting framework, and concludes that WET's value is mission-dependent, that efficiency comparisons require explicit reference planes, and that maturity is highly uneven.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: WET decouples energy generation from energy use and offers strategic advantages for distributed spacecraft, lunar surface networks, mobile assets, and high-power remote loads, motivating a systematic, cross-cutting review that situates diverse WET technologies within a common analytical and accounting structure.

Method: The review surveys near-field inductive and resonant transfer, microwave and millimeter-wave power beaming, optical and laser-based transfer, space-based solar power, lunar and cislunar energy delivery, inter-spacecraft power sharing, intra-spacecraft contactless interfaces, and reconfigurable or relay-assisted routing, all mapped onto a shared R0–R7 energy-accounting framework and assessed across four axes: physical mechanism, energy reference plane, mission architecture, and evidence maturity.

Result: Three findings emerge: (1) WET is most valuable when spatial or temporal mismatches separate generation from load demand, and does not universally replace local generation or onboard storage; (2) dominant loss mechanisms vary across architectures, so beam collection, rectification, receiver output, and useful-load efficiencies are not directly comparable absent explicitly defined energy reference planes; (3) maturity is uneven—high-power ground demonstrations and short-range flight applications are relatively established, while long-range radiative inter-spacecraft transfer, spaceborne routing hardware, lunar energy networks, and deep-space applications rest mainly on models, simulations, and mission studies.

Conclusion: Advancing WET requires validated end-to-end energy budgets under mission-realistic constraints, including pointing and tracking, thermal management, environmental exposure, reliability, energy storage, and regulatory compliance.

Abstract: Wireless energy transfer (WET) decouples energy generation from energy use, offering strategic advantages for distributed spacecraft, lunar surface networks, mobile assets, and high-power remote loads. This review organizes the field along four axes: physical mechanism, energy reference plane, mission architecture, and evidence maturity. It examines near field inductive and resonant transfer, microwave and millimeter wave power beaming, optical and laser-based transfer, space-based solar power, lunar and cislunar energy delivery, inter-spacecraft power sharing, intraspacecraft contactless interfaces, and reconfigurable or relay assisted routing within a common R0,R7 energy-accounting framework. Three conclusions emerge. First, WET is most valuable when spatial or temporal mismatches separate energy generation from load demand; it does not universally replace local generation or onboard storage. Second, dominant losses vary across system architectures. Beam collection, rectification, receiver output, and useful load efficiencies are therefore not directly comparable without explicitly defined energy reference planes. Third, technological maturity is uneven. High power ground demonstrations and short range flight applications are relatively well established, whereas long-range radiative inter spacecraft transfer, spaceborne routing hardware, lunar energy networks, and deep space applications remain supported primarily by models, simulations, and mission studies rather than hardware demonstrations. Progress requires validated end to end energy budgets under mission realistic constraints, including pointing and tracking, thermal management, environmental exposure, reliability, energy storage, and regulatory compliance.

</details>


### [256] [Progress in Cavitated Front Gridline Ag Paste for PERC Solar Cell Architecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17341)
*Donald Intal,Sandra Huneycutt,Abasifreke Ebong,Dana Hankey,Marshall Tibbetts*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: Cavitated silver paste on PERC cells was characterized electrically and microscopically, showing that fill-factor loss (~3.6 pp) stems from resistive current extraction caused by nonuniform interfacial conduction at the metal contact.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Series resistance losses limit PERC cell performance, and cavitated silver paste metallization is explored to reduce these losses; the underlying resistive mechanisms need to be identified.

Method: Fifteen 252 cm2 PERC cells were fabricated with cavitated silver paste. Electrical characterization (fill factor, pseudo-fill factor, series resistance, Voc, Isc) was combined with electroluminescence imaging, cross-sectional STEM, SEM, and conductive AFM after sequential removal of bulk metal and glass.

Result: Mean efficiency 21.87% (max 22.07%); mean fill factor 79.98% vs. pseudo-fill factor 83.60% (deficit 3.62 pp). Fill factor decreased with area-normalized series resistance (0.53–0.83 Ω·cm2) while Voc and Isc stayed constant. Imaging showed localized emission nonuniformity, variable glass interfacial layer, micrometer/sub-micrometer Ag particles, and spatially heterogeneous current response.

Conclusion: Resistive current extraction is the main contributor to fill-factor variation. Interfacial heterogeneity and the connectivity of conducting pathways are key priorities for understanding and reducing extraction losses in cavitated silver metallization.

Abstract: This paper reports on the progress made with the cavitated silver paste on passivated emitter rear contact cells (PERC) to reduce the series resistance. Thus, electrical and microscopy characterizations are used to examine these resistive losses. Fifteen 252 cm2 PERC cells were fabricated for this assessment. Under standard test conditions, these cells achieved a mean conversion efficiency of 21.87% and a maximum of 22.07%. The mean measured fill factor was 79.98%, compared with a pseudo-fill factor of 83.60%, giving a mean deficit of 3.62 percentage points. Fill factor decreased with increasing area-normalized series resistance, which ranged from 0.53 to 0.83 ohm-cm2, while open-circuit voltage and short-circuit current remained unvaried. These electrical trends support resistive current extraction as the principal contributor to fill-factor variation. Electroluminescence imaging revealed localized emission nonuniformity. Cross-sectional scanning transmission electron microscopy showed variation in the glass interfacial layer, while scanning electron microscopy identified micrometer and sub micrometer silver particles. Conductive atomic force microscopy after sequential removal of the bulk metal and glass revealed spatially heterogeneous current response. These observations are consistent with nonuniform electrical access at the contact, where restricted conducting pathways may concentrate on current and increase local voltage drops. The combined characterization identifies interfacial heterogeneity and the connectivity of conducting pathways as priorities for understanding and reducing extraction losses in cavitated silver metallization.

</details>


### [257] [Device Model Linking Singlet and Triplet Spin-State Properties to Organic Photovoltaic Device Characteristics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18022)
*Jun Du,Liming Liu,Jun Yan*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: An exciton-kinetics coupled drift-diffusion (EKDD) model links charge transport with singlet/triplet kinetics, identifying the CT-CS energy offset as the key overlooked factor governing the charge-generation/voltage-loss trade-off in organic photovoltaics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Device-level charge transport and excited-state (singlet/triplet) kinetics in organic photovoltaics are typically modeled separately, leaving the coupled physical mechanisms that govern charge generation, recombination, and voltage loss inadequately described.

Method: The authors develop an exciton-kinetics coupled drift-diffusion (EKDD) model that self-consistently solves state populations and reversible photophysical transitions, bridging device-level charge transport with singlet and triplet kinetics for transient and steady-state calculations. They analyze how LE-CT, CT-CS, and LE-T energy offsets and triplet-transfer kinetics control charge generation, recombination, voltage loss, and extraction, and validate against PM6:Y6 and D18:NFA blends.

Result: The CT-CS offset is identified as a primary, previously overlooked determinant of charge generation and voltage loss: positive offsets make CT3-CS separation exothermic (improving generation but lowering CS energy and increasing voltage loss), while negative offsets make it endothermic and highly temperature-sensitive. The model reproduces triplet-mediated recombination in PM6:Y6 and material-dependent triplet recycling in D18:NFA blends, yielding design rules via LE-CT-CS and T-CT3-CS pathways.

Conclusion: CT-CS alignment is the main determinant of the charge-generation/voltage-loss trade-off, while triplet-recycling kinetics (recycling factor K > ~10^-2) determines whether this balance survives at the device level. Optimal design favors a moderate LE-CT offset (~0.15-0.20 eV) and a weakly exothermic CT-CS offset (~0.05-0.075 eV) with small CT-T offset (≤~0.1 eV). The framework provides a unified description from exciton generation to free charge collection.

Abstract: This work proposes an exciton-kinetics coupled drift-diffusion (EKDD) model that bridges device-level charge transport with singlet and triplet excited-state kinetics. State populations and reversible photophysical transitions are solved self-consistently, enabling transient and steady-state device response calculations. We investigate how LE-CT, CT-CS, and LE-T energy offsets, together with triplet-transfer kinetics, govern charge generation, recombination, voltage loss, and extraction in organic photovoltaics. We identify CT-CS offset as a primary, largely overlooked factor controlling charge generation and voltage loss. A positive offset makes CT3-CS separation exothermic and improves generation, but lowers CS energy and increases voltage loss; a negative offset makes separation endothermic and highly temperature-sensitive. The model reproduces triplet-mediated recombination in PM6:Y6 and material-dependent triplet recycling in D18:NFA blends. We propose design rules for balanced generation and voltage loss via two charge-transfer channels: the LE-CT-CS pathway favors a moderate LE-CT offset of about 0.15-0.20 eV and a weakly exothermic CT-CS offset of about 0.05-0.075 eV; the T-CT3-CS pathway requires the same CT-CS alignment plus a small CT-T offset, no more than about 0.1 eV, corresponding to a recycling factor K greater than about 10^-2, to maintain fast T-to-CT3 recycling. CT-CS alignment is the main determinant of the charge-generation-voltage-loss trade-off, while triplet-recycling kinetics determines whether this balance survives at the device level. This work provides a unified theoretical description from exciton generation to free charge collection, offering a convenient tool for transient and steady-state analysis in organic photovoltaics.

</details>


### [258] [Stability and Optimal Design of Adaptive Linear Bus Holding on Open Chains and Loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18253)
*Li Zhen,Weihua Gu,Miny Shen,Wenbo Fan,Feng Xiao*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: This paper builds a unified analytical framework for adaptive linear bus holding on finite open chains and loops (and relates them to bi-infinite systems), deriving stability conditions, variance/covariance formulas, and closed-form Pareto-optimal designs for schedule- vs headway-holding tradeoffs, validated numerically against four established controls.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bus holding suppresses bunching, but its stability and performance depend on the feedback structure and on how the bus sequence is bounded (open chain vs. loop). The literature lacks a common framework linking finite open chains, loops, and the bi-infinite systems already studied, and it is unclear when periodic closure alters stability, when coefficient-sum stability tests are conservative, and why a stable open chain can still accumulate substantial variance.

Method: The authors develop a common analytical framework for adaptive linear holding on finite open chains and loops, relating both to the bi-infinite system. Matrix and Fourier methods yield necessary and sufficient stability conditions for current- and leading-bus kernels on open chains and general cyclic kernels on loops, plus computable covariance bounds on open chains and exact variance formulas on loops. For loops with same-lap feedback, modal analysis links control coefficients to fleet-wide schedule drift and relative headway patterns.

Result: The analysis shows when periodic closure changes stability, when coefficient-sum stability tests are conservative, and why a stable open chain can still accumulate substantial variance before boundary effects arrive. Closed-form Pareto-optimal designs for schedule- and headway-holding tradeoffs are obtained, with cross-lap output corrections relating them to physical headway and holding performance. Numerical experiments show the analytical design and a three-tap approximation (from closed-form coefficients and a one-dimensional search) outperform four established controls at tested moderate and loose headway-variance budgets, with holding variances within 0.35% and 3.5% of the full cyclic numerical frontier.

Conclusion: The framework provides a tractable design procedure and explains how feedback structure and boundary conditions shape service reliability and holding effort.

Abstract: Bus holding can suppress bunching, but its stability and performance depend on the feedback structure and on how the bus sequence is bounded. This paper develops a common analytical framework for adaptive linear holding on finite open chains and loops and relates both to the bi-infinite system studied in the literature. Matrix and Fourier methods yield necessary and sufficient stability conditions for current- and leading-bus kernels on open chains and general cyclic kernels on loops, together with computable covariance bounds on open chains and exact variance formulas on loops. The analysis shows when periodic closure changes stability, when coefficient-sum stability tests in the literature are conservative, and why a stable open chain can still accumulate substantial variance before boundary effects arrive. For loops with same-lap feedback, modal analysis links control coefficients to fleet-wide schedule drift and to relative headway patterns, explaining the behavior of existing rules and yielding closed-form Pareto-optimal designs for schedule-holding and headway-holding tradeoffs. Cross-lap output corrections relate these designs to physical headway and holding performance. Numerical experiments show that the analytical design and a three-tap approximation, both obtained from closed-form coefficients and a one-dimensional search, outperform four established controls at the tested moderate and loose headway-variance budgets, with holding variances within 0.35% and 3.5% of the full cyclic numerical frontier across these budgets and other parameter variations. The framework thus provides a tractable design procedure and an explanation of how feedback structure and boundary conditions shape service reliability and holding effort.

</details>
